# CIVENSA — DECISIONS LOG

Append-only. Newest entry at the bottom. Never rewrite an old entry; a later decision may supersede it and must say which one.
Entry format: `D-### (date, who) — decision. Reason. Supersedes: …` with facts tagged DOĞRULANDI / TARİHSEL / BEYAN / BELİRSİZ.

## History before D-205
Decisions D-001 to D-204 are in Google Drive, `AI-Work-System/projects/civensa`, documents "CIVENSA_DECISIONS_LOG" … "CIVENSA_DECISIONS_LOG 84" (TARİHSEL, read-only archive). Read them only when an old decision is questioned.

Still in force and needed day to day (summaries; the Drive entry is the full text):
- D-137 — parallel tasks must not touch the same files; each package lists forbidden files.
- D-167 — a PR must name every existing test it changes and why.
- D-177 — plan conversion is a live-session item with the owner (time set by owner).
- D-191 — DeepSeek may be an optional first-pass reviewer for large PRs.
- D-194 — DeepSeek is backup lead with limited authority (`BACKUP_LEAD.md`).
- D-196 — tool acceptance criteria (`HIERARCHY.md` §8); gitleaks blocking, actionlint warning; no new MCP servers or document libraries; ruff/mypy deferred. Its item K5 ("Drive canonical") is superseded by D-205.
- D-197 — PR #84 code accepted; merge when both test shards are green on the head and proof links + measured minutes are in the PR text.
- D-198 — terminal dispatch setup (local CLIs + scripts) rejected. Its reason (3) "repo state files = second source of truth" is superseded by D-205: the repo becomes the only source, Drive is archive.
- D-199 / D-201 — DeepSeek on standby; channel = its Mac workspace (CLAUDE-BRIEF.md, GUNCEL-DURUM.md, YEDEK-KAYIT.md); no Composio, no separate Google account.
- D-202 — hierarchy v1.3: lead merges into phase5 under conditions, sends "@codex" PR comments, scheduled run "Civensa gece kontrol".
- D-203 — PR #85 (CIV-087) not mergeable: MEDIUM login `?message=` reflection; LOW anonymous error-page form, resubmit drops files; test(18,0) failed. Follow-ups posted as "@codex" comments.
- D-204 — PR #84 is draft; lead cannot un-draft via REST; owner marks it ready.

## D-205 (24 Oct 2026 03:24, owner approved; designed by lead) — lead records move from Drive into the repository
Decision: Civensa project rules, hierarchy, backup-lead brief, current state, decisions log and new task packages live in `docs/lead/` on `feature/authorization-workflow-phase5`. Drive keeps the global constitution and the read-only archive.
Reason (DOĞRULANDI 24 Oct): Drive documents cannot be edited in place through the lead's tools, so every update created a new document — the decisions log reached 84 separate files and the state 77 versions; each session spent effort searching for the newest one and risked reading an old one. In git, one file per record, every change visible and reversible, and Codex reads the same files. Suggested by the owner's contact Erhan Meydan (BEYAN, 23 Oct); the parts kept are shared repository + file-based records; terminal CLIs and "lead reads only 5-line summaries" are not adopted (D-198 stands; reviewing diffs found the D-203 MEDIUM that a summary would have hidden).
Safeguards: only `lead/...` branches may change `docs/lead/**` or `AGENTS.md` (CI guard); AGENTS.md tells coding agents not to edit them; no secrets in these files.
Supersedes: D-196 K5, D-198 reason (3), hierarchy v1.3 §6 "Drive wins". Rule docs bumped: HIERARCHY v1.4, PROJECT_RULES v1.3, BACKUP_LEAD v2.
Rule 35 sync still open: civensa-lead skill text, scheduled task prompt, DeepSeek mirror — see STATE.md queue item 8.

## D-207 (24 Oct 2026 ~03:45, lead) — guard runs from the trusted base revision
Codex review of #86 (P1, on b5231a4) accepted: under `pull_request` a PR runs its own copy of the guard and could rewrite it to pass. Fix: `lead-docs-guard.yml` now uses `pull_request_target` (base branch's copy, no checkout, metadata only, branch name passed through env) and also rejects fork PRs.
DOĞRULANDI: branch protection is not available on this private repo without GitHub Pro (REST 403), so no check can block a merge; the guard is a signal and the real gate is that only the lead merges into phase5 and never merges a red PR. Not worth paying for Pro for this alone.
Side effect: `pull_request_target` runs the base branch's file, which does not exist on phase5 until #86 merges, so #86 itself shows no guard run; the first lead PR after #86 is the guard's live test. "@codex review" requested again on the new head before merge.

## D-209 (24 Oct 2026 ~03:50, lead) — lead mistake: turned off Codex full access mid-task
What failed: the lead told the owner to turn off "Tam erişim" in the running CIV-087 task; the session approval policy became "never" and Codex could not push d722e49 or update PR #85; switching to "Onay iste" did not help on retry (DOĞRULANDI, owner screenshots 03:47/03:49).
Why: the lead gave a settings instruction for a running task without knowing how the Codex app applies permission changes mid-session (constitution rule 7/8).
Correct approach: full access back on for this task until the push is done. Prevention: no permission changes on a running Codex task; a permission setup is decided once, tested on a new task in daytime, then recorded.

## D-219 (24 Oct 2026 ~22:35, lead) — CI cost: raise budget now, CIV-091 to cut minutes
DOĞRULANDI (Actions API, October): 237 "Performance program validation" runs; pytest takes 37–43 min per shard, 2 shards per push (~80 job-minutes); other workflows are negligible. Owner screenshot: $52 gross metered, $12 included, Actions budget 100% used. Concurrency cancel and docs path filter already exist, so the cost driver is suite duration × pushes (Codex draft iterations). Decision: owner raises the Actions budget (about $80/month) so work continues; CIV-091 skips tests on draft PRs and cuts suite time without removing tests. Making the repo public (free minutes) is rejected for now: institutional data and security exposure (constitution rule 24).

## D-232 (25 Oct 2026 ~11:45, lead, live session CV Lider 19) — lead mistake: dispatch format and handover
BEYAN (owner, 11:38): no handover note was given for the next session; the CIV-096 dispatch named the model vaguely ("the model you used for CIV-095"); the task title was outside the paste block.
What failed: HIERARCHY dispatch rule (PLATFORM → PROJECT → TITLE → TEXT, model and reasoning level) was applied loosely. Why: the lead did not verify the current Codex model name and skipped the handover because the session closed early.
Correct approach (applies from now, every dispatch and every session end): (1) each dispatch states platform, the exact verified model name and the effort level; (2) the title is the first line INSIDE the paste block; (3) every live session ends with a paste-ready handover note for the next session (title + text in one block, model and effort for the lead session).
DOĞRULANDI (OpenAI ChatGPT & Codex changelog, read 25 Oct): GPT-6.1 Sol is the default Codex model; GPT-5.5 retired from Codex on 14 Oct 2026. Codex dispatches use GPT-6.1 Sol; effort per task (CIV-096: High).

## D-242 (25 Oct 2026 ~21:55, lead, live session CV Lider 23) — Codex cloud environment for "@codex" fixes: not now
Question (owner, 25 Oct): set up a Codex cloud environment so "@codex" fix comments run directly on the PR (D-208 gap).
Source-backed (OpenAI docs, read 25 Oct): a non-review "@codex" PR mention starts a cloud chat on Codex Cloud (Legacy) with the PR as context and can push a fix to the branch when it has permission (learn.chatgpt.com/docs/third-party/github.md). The cloud environments page says Codex Cloud (Legacy) still serves Code Review and the GitHub integration and that OpenAI plans to deprecate it (no date given) (learn.chatgpt.com/docs/environments/cloud-environments). Legacy setup scripts have internet; the agent phase has no internet by default; secrets exist only during setup (learn.chatgpt.com/docs/environments/cloud-environment).
BELİRSİZ: whether a PostgreSQL server can be started in the legacy environment so the test suite runs there (no source found); Playwright screenshot capture there also unverified.
Judgment against HIERARCHY §8: (1) gap is small — today the owner pastes one fix text into the task's Codex conversation; (3) test runnability unverified; (4) reversible; (5) the Codex GitHub app needs push permission on the repo; (6) uses the ChatGPT Pro Codex quota; (7) owner setup work in the Codex web UI. Building on a feature the vendor plans to deprecate is not worth it.
Decision: do not set it up; D-208 flow stays. Re-open only if OpenAI moves PR-mention fixes to the new cloud environments, or fix rounds through the owner become frequent (more than three a week).

## D-246 (25 Oct 2026 ~22:45, owner + lead) — Claude↔Codex link: no OpenAI API; GitHub Copilot cloud agent researched, trial sequenced
BEYAN (owner, 22:16–22:28): Claude and the coding agent must talk directly; no new payment to OpenAI (openai/codex-action needs an OpenAI API key → rejected); proposed GitHub Copilot instead; asked for thorough research first.
Eliminated (lead): Codex CLI from the owner's Mac — the lead's device shell is a Linux VM inside the Claude app, not the Mac terminal; Codex sign-in would not persist across sessions (DOĞRULANDI get_device_info / tool description).
Source-backed (docs.github.com / github.blog, read 25 Oct):
- Cloud agent is in all paid Copilot plans. Pro $10/month = 1,500 AI credits (1,000 base + 500 flex; 1 credit = $0.01), metered by tokens at model rates (GPT-6.1 Sol $2 in / $0.10 cached / $10 out per 1M — same as OpenAI API); resets on the 1st, no carry-over; extra usage only with a budget.
- "@copilot" in a PR comment makes Copilot push commits to that PR's branch; it reacts only to users with write access (lead comments are posted as dursuncavus, DOĞRULANDI). Model: picker only in the web UI, otherwise Auto. Max 59 min per session. Reads AGENTS.md.
- Workflows do not run on Copilot pushes until a write user clicks "Approve and run workflows"; a repo admin setting can skip this, and GitHub warns it can expose Actions secrets. REST approve reportedly returns 403 (community, unverified).
- Environment: .github/workflows/copilot-setup-steps.yml on the default branch, job copilot-setup-steps, `services` allowed (PostgreSQL), setup steps outside the firewall.
Model analysis (BELİRSİZ, not measured): one fix round ≈ $1–3 → Pro covers roughly 5–10 fix rounds a month.
Decision: Copilot Pro trial is the chosen route for fix rounds (Codex app keeps the main tasks, Pro quota). Order: (1) public-repo steps 3–6; (2) D-247 secrets move; (3) owner buys Copilot Pro, additional-usage budget $0, "Require approval for workflow runs" off; (4) lead writes copilot-setup-steps.yml + Copilot notes, "@codex review", owner approves the merge (workflow change); (5) first fix round as the test. If Auto picks a Claude model, review goes to Codex (rule 18). Replaces D-208 for fix rounds once the trial passes; D-242 stands.

## D-247 (25 Oct 2026 ~22:45, lead, security finding, owner approval needed) — repository secrets reachable from any branch
DOĞRULANDI (workflow files): NEON_BACKUP_DATABASE_URL (live database, db-backup.yml) and BACKUP_AGE_IDENTITY (backup decryption key, preprint-backup-rehearsal.yml, release-rehearsal.yml) are repository-level secrets with no environment. A workflow file changed on any branch of this repository and triggered by pull_request can read them. Today Codex branches already run workflows without approval, so the exposure exists before Copilot. Likelihood low (agents follow AGENTS.md, prompts are ours), impact high (live data, backup key). BELİRSİZ: whether the Neon backup role is read-only.
Source-backed: environment secrets and deployment-branch rules need GitHub Pro for private repositories and are free for public ones (docs.github.com, managing environments).
Proposal: right after the repo becomes public (step 6), move both secrets into an environment "production" restricted to feature/authorization-workflow-phase5, and reference it only from the backup/rehearsal jobs. Owner enters the values (lead never sees them); lead changes the workflows, "@codex review", owner approves. Added to the public-repo plan as step 7.

## D-252 (25 Oct 2026 ~23:30, owner question + lead decision) — process: evidence scaled to risk, bigger tasks, no new AI
BEYAN (owner, 23:27): the setup feels amateur; the four AIs are not used efficiently.
Lead analysis (model judgment): the core is sound (PR-only changes, CI as judge, cross-family review, records in the repo). Real waste: (1) the owner relays every task and fix between Claude and Codex; (2) small polish tasks carry heavy evidence — #126 changed ~20 lines of code and added ~6,000 lines of evidence (97-screen capture JSON, scripts), costing Codex time and the lead's scarce quota; (3) tasks cut too small (two LOW polish items = two full cycles).
Decision:
- Evidence by risk. A change limited to one screen's own template/CSS/JS: targeted tests + screenshots of the changed states (1440/390) + contrast of new pairs; no all-screen capture, no capture scripts or summary JSON committed. All-screen capture only when a shared file changes (base.html, shell.css/js, tokens.css, app.css, app.js used by several screens) — then commit one short summary (counts), not per-element JSON.
- Bigger tasks: one Codex task may cover several related screens or findings; LOW findings are batched into the next task on the same screen instead of their own task.
- No new AI participants (rule 17). The relay gap is addressed by the Copilot trial for fix rounds (D-246/D-248).
Applies to packages from CIV-102 on. CIV-102 touches base.html, so its all-screen capture stays, in the short form.

## D-278 (26 Oct 2026 ~18:00, lead) — lead mistake: CIV-105 merged before every CI job finished
What failed: PR #150 was merged (7cb58d9) while job "test (18, 1)" on head 721f2c2 was still in_progress; the wait loop timed out and the merge condition only checked for the word "failure".
Why: merge guard tested for absence of failure instead of presence of success on every job.
Correct approach / prevention: merge only when every check run on the exact head is completed with conclusion success or skipped; a timed-out wait stops without merging. Same rule for lead-record PRs (guard success).
State: "test (18, 0)" success; "test (18, 1)" still in_progress at 18:35 (started 14:51Z, possibly stuck) → next session verifies; if it fails, revert 7cb58d9 and send the fix round. Changes after the last green round were test-only (Copilot acd1372) plus the artifact restore.

BEYAN (owner 10:14): Render security advisory https://render.com/docs/advisory-emudhra (eMudhra intermediate keys compromised, disclosed 19 Oct 2026; Python: certifi ≥ 2026.10.19; redeploy alone not enough; Docker images own their CA store). Source-backed (advisory read 27 Oct).

Owner escalation (merge to main + deploy): merge #177 when its test job is green despite the rehearsal failure, then Render manual deploy; BELİRSİZ whether Render auto-deploys main. Follow-up (LOW): main's rehearsal gate cannot pass until phase5 is promoted — handle at the D-177 release.

## D-292 (27 Oct 2026 ~12:05 Istanbul, owner + lead, live session CV Lider 33) — merge rule v1.5; label-next-to-value rule; CIV-120..123
BEYAN (owner 11:59): "kural onay". HIERARCHY v1.5: the lead may merge a PR into phase5 when it is behind only by commits that change none of the PR's files and CI is green on the PR head; performance-program.yml now also runs on every push to phase5 (job condition extended to push events); a red phase5 run → revert the last merge first. Reason: serial behind-0 merging cost ~30 min CI per PR (5 green PRs ≈ 2.5 h). This PR changes a workflow → "@codex review" before merge.
Design rule (owner 11:18–11:24, adopted for every screen package from now on): a label and its value/status belong together — R1 number-only tiles become compact label/number rows; R2 no far-edge statuses in wide panels (row content ≤ 44rem). Owner examples: 021 progress panel (CIV-121, Copilot issue #182 → PR #183), 022 task list (CIV-122, lead PR #184). CIV-118 (#175) got the same R2 fix by Copilot comment. Sweep of all design-system screens: CIV-123 (Codex, effort High, PR #185).
CIV-120 (043/044 PP kaynak karşılaştırması + status filter): package given to Codex as paste text (to avoid making waiting PRs behind), PR #181. Packages CIV-120, CIV-121, CIV-123 added to docs/lead/tasks/ by this PR.

## D-319 (30 Oct 2026 ~10:20 Istanbul, lead, live session CV Lider 41) — merges; rehearsal fails on the backup decryption key
DOĞRULANDI merges (reviewed in D-318, CI green on the exact head, behind 0): #257 CIV-156 → 2f2276c · #256 CIV-155 (head = reviewed 8f97459 + base merge) → 3544177 · #250 CIV-152 (head = reviewed cd879f6 + base merge) → 5936096. Night record #258 still open (docs only).
Release rehearsal (D-315 step 2, backup run 41013202751 of 30 Oct 06:17Z) run 41015540759 failed after 2 s with the generic "diagnostic details suppressed". Cause of the hidden message: BackupCryptoError is not a RehearsalFailure. Lead PR #259 re-raises it with its fixed Turkish text; "@codex review" no major issues; CI green 907a05c → a4bf3ef. Re-run 41018789348: "Yedek çözülemedi: Yedek şifreleme/çözme başarısız; anahtarı ve dosya bütünlüğünü kontrol edin." The ciphertext pair passed the header check, so the age identity in environment `production` (BACKUP_AGE_IDENTITY) does not decrypt backups made with BACKUP_AGE_RECIPIENT. The identity had never been exercised since D-247 moved it (the D-287 verification run was the backup, which only encrypts). Lead error: D-287 accepted a backup run as proof that both environment secrets are valid; prevention: every secret move is verified by a run that actually uses that secret.
Impact: go-live blocked at step 2 until fixed; also, until verified, no encrypted backup is proven restorable. Owner action: re-enter BACKUP_AGE_IDENTITY in Settings → Environments → production from the Passwords copy (the line starting AGE-SECRET-KEY-1, nothing else); lead re-runs the rehearsal. If it still fails, the saved private key does not belong to the recipient → new key pair + new backups (escalation, next decision).
CIV-153 (#251): "@codex" non-review mentions only trigger a Codex review (confirmed again, as in D-242). Fix rounds went to Copilot ("@copilot" comments 6516462274, 6516615803, 6516843296): table fits at 1440, select readable, then mid-word breaks and a 17rem sticky column found by the lead → third round (no sticky, no mid-word breaks). Review pending.
CIV-154 (#252): shard 0 failed on test_activity_mapping_design populated-after-390 (CIV-150 test, not a CIV-154 file) — treated as flaky until it repeats; update-branch reruns CI.

## D-353 (31 Oct 2026 14:13 Istanbul, owner + lead, live session CV Lider 48) — data collection this year: Civensa-generated forms

DOĞRULANDI (lead, phase5 2ed0893): partial prior art only — Faaliyet Raporu financial section has a blank Excel template download + Excel upload with preview/apply (app/activity_financial_routes.py /template/{kind}, /imports/...); Performans Programı has a Word (docx) importer for the printed book tables (app/import_service.py parse_pp_import). There is no per-department, pre-filled form export that comes back and is matched automatically.
Lead direction (proposed, not accepted yet — scope change, §4): a form round-trip — Civensa exports one form per department pre-filled with what is already known, with hidden record keys; the returned file is uploaded, previewed with Turkish errors per field, and applied. Numbers and tables go in Excel (cells, data validation, protected key columns survive editing; Word tables break on edit); narrative text may stay Word. Research gate (rule 11/14) before the package: existing patterns for template round-trip data collection (openpyxl protection/validation, government data-collection templates).

## D-359 (31 Oct 2026 ~14:50 Istanbul, owner + lead, live session CV Lider 48) — renamed / merged / closed directorates: what the system handles
Owner question (14:40): are we ready for directorates that are renamed, merged or closed (e.g. Afet İşleri Müdürlüğü in 2026 → Afet İşleri ve Risk Yönetimi Müdürlüğü in 2027)? Half-year + deadline answer comes Monday (BEYAN).
DOĞRULANDI (lead, phase5 466482a, app/structure_service.py, app/models.py StructureChange*):
- PP / Faaliyet Raporu: Strateji's "Kurum yapısı" screen records RENAMED, MERGED, SPLIT, CLOSED, CREATED with council decision no/date and effective date. A rename keeps the same department record, so its history stays together; periods before the effective year show the old name (period_name), later ones the new name. Merge/split/close moves chosen indicators/activities to the receiving unit; the closing unit is still required in its closure year's Faaliyet Raporu and excluded after. Used by activity report, financial, sections, unit workflow, assurance, budget, master data.
- İç Kontrol does not use these decisions: (a) rename — actions stay with the unit (same record), period batch label is frozen at period open (correct), but the official monitoring export prints the current name (internal_control_monitoring_export.py:193), so a re-export of an old period would show the new name; (b) merge/close — actions stay assigned to the inactive unit ("named obligation", monitoring_service ~line 108), nobody in the receiving unit gets them; (c) imports match old names only through the one-time alias mapping step (the 8 names, D-331).
- Real case likely: the İç Kontrol mapping list already contains Afet İşleri, Teftiş Kurulu, İnsan Kaynakları Eğitim name changes (tab 2).
Decision: (1) Ask Ali Abi on Monday for the list of directorate changes since 2024 (old name, new name/receiver, council decision no + date, effective date). (2) The lead records them on "Kurum yapısı" with the owner BEFORE the 2026 PP load (CIV-163), so the 2026 book's old names attach to the right units. (3) İç Kontrol gap (b)+(a) becomes a Codex task only if the list contains a merge/split/closure inside the 2024–2026 plan, or before the 2027–2029 plan — otherwise rule 15 (not needed yet). Packaged after Monday's list.
Next IDs: task CIV-163, decision D-360.

- FR5: departments enter indicator actuals in bulk by Excel; Strateji already has a separate Excel per directorate with every indicator listed. → reopens D-356 (direct screen entry). Lead direction (proposal, owner approves — scope change): indicator-actuals Excel import per directorate (USE/ADAPT their existing sheet layout + the existing financial XLSX preview/apply pattern), screen entry stays for corrections and evidence files. Package after one current directorate Excel arrives (rule 11/15).
- ML1: budget actuals come from Mali Hizmetler, usually on an Excel template, sometimes PDF. Existing financial XLSX import is the target; need the template sample.
- ML2: Mali Hizmetler totals are authoritative (kesin hesap in May). Lead direction: report prints Mali Hizmetler figures; a directorate total that differs is a visible Turkish warning with the amount, not a block. Package after reading the financial gate code (next session).
- ML3: Mali Hizmetler sections stay with Mali Hizmetler (matches code).
- PR1: pilot directorates Bilgi İşlem, Strateji Geliştirme, Fen İşleri; accounts opened after talking to them.
- PR2: approval order: directorate Harcama Yetkilisi → Ali Kaya → Başkan Yardımcıları → Üst Yönetici. The system has directorate submit → Strateji approve; the last two steps are not in the system. Open question to Ali: do the Başkan Yardımcısı and Üst Yönetici steps need to be in Civensa, or on paper/signature as today? Not built until answered (rule 15).
Task numbering: CIV-163 is used for these three small changes; the 2026 year-parameterised loader becomes CIV-164.

## D-372 (3 Nov 2026 ~13:55 Istanbul, lead, live session CV Lider 51) — 2026 PP load rule; CIV-165 package
Session start: Gmail — no Ali Abi mail after 2 Nov 20:05 UTC. Open PRs: only #321 (CIV-167). No other lead PR (no duplicate session).
CIV-167 review (#321 head f36ab4e, against tasks/CIV-167.md, DOĞRULANDI): name_for_year factored out of period_name (period_name output unchanged); new batch labels use the period year; Word/Excel party names: frozen batch label → name_for_year; monitoring screen "unassigned" list uses name_for_year (template reads .name, dict works); 6 PostgreSQL tests cover acceptance 1–3 + DepartmentScope priority; only allowed files; no migration. 2026 book facts (DOĞRULANDI on the committed approved IDML, sha256 1aaa0bb8…f33d6): 105 cards / 226 indicators (no SH/PG codes, header 2025 Planlanan … 2028 Tahmin), 33 cost, 33 economic, 111 activity description tables, no membership tables; Ministry guidance rows inside cards; ~20 pages with two cards; directorate spelling variants, the "Sorumlu İdare" row always clean; cost activities without codes. Only 37/226 indicator names equal a 2027 name (SH/PG stripped), 26 of those with equal 2026 target.
Decision (2026↔2027 matching rule):
1. Directorates match by the "Sorumlu İdare" row through the owner-confirmed name_map (D-94) plus five lead-decided spelling/rename entries (Afet İşleri ve Risk Değerlendirme → Afet İşleri ve Risk Yönetimi; Strateji → Strateji Geliştirme; Etüt → Etüd Proje; Park Bahçeler → Park ve Bahçeler; Ruhsat Denetim → Ruhsat ve Denetim). Yapı Kontrol → the existing department. Anything else unmatched → refusal; the 2026 load creates no department.
2. 2026 names print in 2026 output through DepartmentScope.display_name of the 2026 period (period_name already prefers it); no Kurum yapısı decision is written for this. This also covers the D-359 renames for the 2026 PP/FR (Basın Yayın ve Halkla İlişkiler, Sosyal Hizmetler, Harita ve Planlama, Afet İşleri ve Risk Değerlendirme).
3. Indicators: NO automatic cross-year identity link (match rate too low; system rule "no name/code matching" stays). The 2026 period is self-contained — the 2026 Faaliyet Raporu needs only 2026 targets. The loader writes a candidate report (exact normalised name, same directorate); linking is a later Strategy action with a reason, needed only for the 2028 PP history columns.
4. Activities: description tables become the 2026 official catalogue; cost activities link to it only on a unique exact name match, the rest listed, not guessed.
Reason: reversible, uses existing mechanisms, keeps 2027 untouched; no owner/Ali input needed. Package docs/lead/tasks/CIV-165.md dispatched to Codex (GPT-6.1 Sol). After its PR: lead review → rehearsal on a disposable DB → backup → owner live load (D-357).

## D-379 (3 Nov 2026 ~16:10 Istanbul, owner + lead, live session CV Lider 52) — CIV-170 head-to-head: DeepSeek vs Gemini (Jules); scoring fixed before results
BEYAN (owner 16:03): DeepSeek is already working on CIV-170; give the same task to Gemini too and compare — fully objective, no "political" decision.

Comparison adds real value (rule 17: choosing a lane), cost is one small task. Neither is Claude's model family, so the lead reviews both.
Precondition: Jules needs GitHub write access; main/staging/phase5 have NO branch protection (DOĞRULANDI 3 Nov ~16:00, API 404 "Branch not protected"). Jules is connected only after a ruleset (PR required, no force push, no deletion) is in place — owner approval needed (security setting).
Scoring (fixed now, before either result is seen; 100 points; ties broken by fewer review findings):
1. Scope & rules (20): only scripts/first_load_live.py + tests changed; AGENTS.md hard rules kept; no secrets; Turkish texts. −10 per violation.
2. CI (25): full CI green on first pushed commit = 25; green after one fix round = 12; more = 0.
3. Acceptance 1–5 of tasks/CIV-170.md (25): 5 points each, checked by the lead in the diff and tests.
4. Lead review findings (20): 20 − 5 per MEDIUM − 10 per HIGH (LOW free).
5. Honest report (10): RESULT/PR text claims only what was run; −10 if any claim is false.
Not scored: speed (different queues), diff size. Owner effort is noted, not scored.
Delivery: DeepSeek → workspace civ170/ patch, lead pushes branch task/civ-170-deepseek; Jules → its own branch, PR title "CIV-170 — … (Jules)". Each scored separately by the lead with the same checklist, written into this log before the merge decision. Only the winner is merged; the other PR is closed with its score. Result decides the third coding lane in hierarchy v1.6 §2 e (DeepSeek or Jules).

## D-411 (4 Nov 2026 ~21:30 Istanbul, owner + lead, live session CV Lider 61) — presentation module ("Sunum") architecture; future report modules proposed
Session start (DOĞRULANDI): no mail from Ali Abi; only open PR #382 (parked); the 18:03Z db-backup failure mail is the refused main dispatch already covered by D-410.
Proposed, not accepted (rule 32) — report modules after the 1 Jan 2027 roadmap, from bcekmece.bel.tr "Yayınlar" (DOĞRULANDI 4 Nov): (1) Kurumsal Mali Durum ve Beklentiler Raporu (5018 art. 30, every July; reuses PP activities + Mali Hizmetler budget actuals ML1) → target July 2027; (2) Stratejik Plan 2025–2029 monitoring/evaluation as an extension of FR; (3) Kamu Hizmet Standartları Tablosu + Kamu Hizmet Envanteri inside İç Kontrol; (4) action-plan tracking (İklim/SECAP 2024, Yerel Eşitlik) on the İç Kontrol action engine, low; (5) Sayıştay finding tracking, low. Not in Civensa: meclis agendas/decisions/commission reports (Yazı İşleri / document system), budget preparation (accounting software; Civensa reads only), vision document, legislation list. Owner asks Ali on 9 Nov who prepares which and how long it takes.
BEYAN (owner 21:25): every report needs presentations; build a module for them. Earlier owner rules (TARİHSEL, pre-D-205 memory): PDF only (no PowerPoint, numbers must not be editable), 16:9 on purpose although the hall screen is 4:3, no special design layer per presentation; müdürlükler present the FR to the mayor, Strateji presents reports to the council.
Decision (lead): one shared Sunum engine for all report modules, not one per report.
- Engine: ReportLab (already in requirements.txt, used by ar_pdf_export.py) with a 16:9 page; charts with reportlab.graphics. USE before BUILD; no new rendering dependency.
- Fixed slide types: kapak, bölüm, özet rakamlar, tablo, grafik (bar/çizgi), madde metni, kapanış. Long tables split across slides automatically, never shrunk below a minimum font size.
- Each report type has a "sunum tarifi" (which slides, which data). Numbers come only from the approved/published snapshot; each slide footer shows source report + date; nothing is typed by hand in the presentation.
- Scope filter: one müdürlük (→ mayor) or the whole municipality (→ council).
- Look: the owner designs one 16:9 master once (colours, fonts, the 7 slide types); the code applies it to every presentation.
- Order: (1) FR müdürlük presentation (part of the FR module scope), (2) FR council presentation, (3) PP, then the new report modules as they arrive.
- Next: owner sends the master design → lead writes CIV-180 (engine + FR müdürlük recipe) for Codex. Ask Ali on 9 Nov: when do müdürlükler present the FR to the mayor (sets the CIV-180 deadline).
Next IDs: task CIV-180, decision D-412.

## D-412 (4 Nov 2026 ~21:40 Istanbul, owner + lead, live session CV Lider 61) — Sunum: animated HTML (reveal.js) + PDF from the same source; supersedes the D-411 engine line
BEYAN (owner 21:34, Ali Abi's request): small animations in presentations, Prezi-style if possible.
Checked: Prezi itself is commercial and manual (numbers typed by hand) → rejected (no commercial software; numbers only from Civensa). reveal.js is MIT open source with Auto-Animate (morph between slides), fragments (step-by-step reveal), overview mode and PDF printing (DOĞRULANDI, project README).
Decision (lead), replaces D-411 "Engine: ReportLab": Civensa generates ONE self-contained HTML presentation file (reveal.js bundled inside, works offline, no internet in the hall needed). The same file prints to the 16:9 PDF in the browser (print-pdf mode) → no Chromium on the Render server. ReportLab is not used for presentations.
- "Prezi feel" without Prezi: an overview map slide (sections as tiles) → zoom/morph into a section → back to the map; numbers count up, bars grow, rows appear step by step. Motion is short (≤1 s) and calm; full Prezi-style spinning/zooming canvas is NOT built (distracting for a council audience, hard to lay out automatically from data).
- Every animation degrades to a still slide in the PDF; the PDF stays the official copy (archive, e-mail, council file).
- Numbers: HTML is text and could be edited by hand → each presentation carries a short verification code (snapshot hash) on the closing slide and in the footer; Civensa can show the code for any approved report. Risk lowered, not zero (BELİRSİZ whether that is enough for Ali → ask 9 Nov).
- D-411 slide types, recipes, scope filter, order and the owner's master design are unchanged; the owner's master design adds where motion goes (one note per slide type).
Next IDs: task CIV-180, decision D-413.

## D-413 (4 Nov 2026 ~21:45 Istanbul, owner + lead, live session CV Lider 61) — Sunum: owner answers, engine pinned, design prototype
BEYAN (owner 21:39): the presentation computer has internet; a verification code on the slides is enough against hand-edited numbers (closes the two D-412 questions for Ali). Owner: "don't reinvent" — find an existing engine and design.
Research (DOĞRULANDI 4 Nov, web + upstream repos): reveal.js 6.0.1 (MIT, 11 Apr 2026; Auto-Animate, fragments, overview, print-pdf) — chosen. Slidev rejected (Node/Vite build per deck; Civensa is Python). Quarto rejected (extra CLI binary on the server). Marp rejected (little animation). Prezi / Gamma / Beautiful.ai rejected (commercial, numbers typed by hand). Charts: Apache ECharts 6.1.0 (Apache-2.0; animated bars, valueAnimation counts numbers up). Commercial PPT/Keynote templates rejected (license is for editing in PowerPoint, not for re-implementation in our code).
Decision (lead): Civensa renders the deck with its existing Jinja templates → reveal.js + ECharts files served by Civensa (also bundled into the downloadable file); no build step, no new server binary. Design base = Civensa's own design tokens (app/static/design/tokens.css: accent #1e6b65, Fira Sans, OFL, already in the repo) so presentations match the app; no external template.
Prototype (lead, synthetic data, labelled ÖRNEK VERİ): https://claude.ai/artifact/DMpNp2f68JsogL2eMgJSRD — 11 slides covering all 7 slide types + the map: cover, map (tile → zoom into section), section, KPI count-up, ECharts bar chart, table rows step by step with total last, bullets, closing slide with verification code; design notes per slide (toggle). Checked locally in Chromium: no console errors, all slides rendered.
Change to D-411 owner item: the owner no longer designs the master from zero; he reviews/corrects the prototype (colours, type, layout, motion) → lead writes CIV-180 from the approved prototype.
Next IDs: task CIV-180, decision D-414.

## D-421 (5 Nov 2026 ~09:50 Istanbul, owner + lead, live session CV Lider 62) — meclis presentation target set above the previous one; research findings
BEYAN (owner 09:33): the previous meclis presentation cannot be our target; we must do better than it. → D-420 "owner sends the previous presentation" withdrawn.
Research (lead, 5 Nov; sources checked):
- Public-sector data storytelling (ZHAW/World Bank CFRR guide): good examples tell the year as a story (City of Toronto annual report), use many images to create emotion (Auckland Council summary), short consistent key messages (Singapore MoF leaflet, GovEx one-pagers). Canadian municipal guide: infographics (Chatham-Kent), quotes/examples (Calgary), strategy-vs-measure updates (Fernie).
- Turkish practice (news, Apr 2026): in several municipalities the mayor presents the FR with a detailed presentation of completed investments before the council vote (Sultangazi, İBB) → BELİRSİZ who presents at Büyükçekmece (D-411 said Strateji); ask Ali 9 Nov.
- Storage: Neon Free database storage is 0.5 GB per project (some sources: raised to 1 GB end Sep 2026 — BELİRSİZ which applies to our project). DB backup artifact today 2.8 MB compressed. Photos in PostgreSQL would also inflate every backup → not in the database. Candidates: Neon Object Storage (same account, S3-compatible, Free 5 GB/project, 5 GB/month egress shared with Postgres; GA status BELİRSİZ) — preferred USE option; Cloudflare R2 (10 GB free, zero egress) as fallback. Decision after a short verification, before the photo task.
Target (lead proposal, owner reviews): the meclis deck tells the year in 3–5 key messages chosen by Strateji. Slide types to add: spot (one big number from report data + short sentence), full-screen photo with caption, önce/sonra photo comparison (slider in HTML, side by side in PDF), "öne çıkan iş" card per müdürlük (photo + one number + one sentence), strategic-goal scorecard (target vs actual, status colour), budget distribution (where 1 TL goes, from economic data). Numbers always from approved data + verification code; texts by Strateji; photos by müdürlükler (caption, date, mahalle, linked activity). Single-file size limit for this recipe must rise above CIV-180's 3 MB (photos resized, e.g. WebP ~1600 px).
Order: CIV-182 continues (numeric backbone) → owner designs the new slide types once → storage verification + decision → photo upload/storage task → content editor (spot/öne çıkan iş selection) + new slide types task.
Next IDs: task CIV-183, decision D-422.

## D-430 (6 Nov 2026 ~12:50–13:55 Istanbul, lead, live session CV Lider 67) — CIV-186 reviewed, two lead fixes, merged
DOĞRULANDI: Gmail (1 day) — no mail from Ali Abi (only Earth Studio render notices and unrelated mail).
DOĞRULANDI #413 CIV-186 (Codex, head 217c707) against tasks/CIV-186.md: allowed files only; pagination stays in Python; CI green. Defect found by the lead from the PR's own screenshots: every wrapped line was charged as a whole row and .65 em per character was used, so pages were half empty (33-row summary → 8 pages of ~4 rows; scorecard 2 rows/page; long bullets 1/page).
Lead fix 9920c9d: an extra line costs a fraction of a row (table .6 = 35/64 px, bullets .6), CHAR_EM .58, bullets budget 7 units (5-item maximum unchanged). "@codex review" → P2 (valid): meclis.css switches scorecards to the compact layout only at 6 rows, so a split page has taller rows (5 full rows with two wrapped names end at 984 px, footer 971).
Lead fix 6322759: scorecards paged by pixel height measured in Chrome (full row 104 + 36.4 per extra name line, compact 81 + 31.2, gap 14; limit 590 px with the "Tablo n/N" counter, 625 px for a single-page slide), largest group that fits. Regression: unit test (2 wrapped + 4 short) + browser slide "Karma karne". Local (Chrome, normal + #pdf): 15 passed; every last row above the footer. Short-row decks byte-identical to the old pagination (monkeypatched legacy check, CI).
"@codex review" on 6322759: no major issues. CI green (run 42199880438), behind 0 → merged 00699ae.
Result: 33-row İdare summary 8 → 6 pages; real-name scorecards 6 per page. Live after the next release (no migration). The 7-rows-per-page workaround in docs/lead/data/sunum-2025-deneme-build.py can go when the test deck is rebuilt.

## D-443 (9 Nov 2026 ~09:55 Istanbul, lead, project thread CV Lider 71) — Ali's attachments read; FR5 Excel findings; FR5 package after today's meeting
BEYAN (owner 09:41): pasted Ali's answers B–E and uploaded the 3 attachments as a zip in the project thread. DOĞRULANDI: pasted text = D-442 line by line, no new figure; section A (mapping) and the İ2 "İKİY" row still not answered.
DOĞRULANDI lead read of "2026 Performans Değerlendirme.xlsx" (sheet "veriler", plus ANALİZ and 4 chart sheets):
- 227 indicator rows, 32 directorates, one master workbook for all directorates (Strateji's sheet, not one file per directorate as D-361 assumed).
- Columns: Ana hizmet alanı, stratejik hedef + SH kodu, sorumlu müdürlük, PG kodu, gösterge, hesaplama yöntemi (TOPLA 122 / KÜMÜLATİF 105, spelling varies), birim, hedef etkisi, 2025 hedef, 2025 gerçekleşen, 2026 hedef, Q1–Q4 2026, Q4 total, 2026 yıl toplamı, değerlendirme formulas, sapma kodu, açıklama.
- Q1–Q3 filled (4 Rehberlik ve Teftiş rows blank in Q2/Q3); Q4, "2026 YILI TOPLAMI", sapma kodu and açıklama empty; the evaluation formulas divide the empty year total by the target. Ratios are stored as fractions (1 = %100).
- PG codes are NOT unique (they restart per stratejik hedef, e.g. SH01/PG01 appears 21 times) → a row key is (müdürlük, SH kodu, PG kodu) or the name, never the PG code alone.
- Against the loaded 2026 book (226 indicators, CIV-165 list): 155 same name, 7 close name, 17 no match in the same directorate, 48 rows under 4 directorate names that differ from the book (Harita ve Planlama, Sosyal Hizmetler, Basın Yayın ve Halkla İlişkiler, Destek Hizmetler vs book Plan Proje, Yapı Kontrol, Sosyal Destek, Halkla İlişkiler, Destek Hizmetleri); 63 book indicators found no Excel row. Some same-directorate rows also differ in name and target (e.g. Sağlık İşleri "Toplum sağlığı ... eğitim ve seminer sayısı" 5 vs book "... eğitimleri ve danışmanlık hizmetleri sayısı" 7). Matching file (no actual values, PP names/targets only): docs/lead/data/fr5-2026-excel-kitap-eslesme.csv.
- V2 PDFs: "2025 Bütçe Kesin Gider Hesabı" (19 p., kurum/fonksiyonel/ekonomik kod per directorate, text PDF) and "2025 Yılı Gelir Kesin Hesap" (1 p.). Final accounts, not the ML1 import template (D-442 stands).
Decisions (lead):
1. FR5 is an import by Strateji of its master workbook (one upload, all directorates), not per-directorate files; screen entry stays for corrections and evidence. The FR actual is one annual value per indicator → the import reads the year value (TOPLA: sum of quarters; KÜMÜLATİF: last filled quarter) only when Strateji says the year is closed; Q1–Q3 are shown in the preview for checking, not stored as the FR actual.
2. Rows are bound to Civensa indicators through a reviewed mapping (preview → Strateji confirms → apply), never by PG code alone; the name differences above make an automatic-only match unsafe.
3. CIV-192 (FR5 import) is packaged after today's meeting, because two Ali answers change it: (a) directorate name changes since 2024 (A9) — 48 rows depend on it; (b) is this Excel the one directorates fill at year end, or do they send their rows to Strateji? The raw workbook is not committed (internal 9-month actuals, rule 24); it stays in the project thread upload.

## D-470 (11 Nov 2026 ~19:45 Istanbul, lead, CV Lider 73 project thread) — CIV-217: 2027 replace guards adjusted on live evidence
- Owner's live read-only CIV-214 plan (release #489): 341 refusals, 0 rows written (DOĞRULANDI, output uploaded by the owner). BEYAN (owner): no 2027 activity was entered.
- Root causes (DOĞRULANDI, two owner-run read-only inspections, 0 rows written, plus a local reproduction of the 30 Oct loader at 310546c): (1) the D-135 preprint clear kept 2027 programmes/subprogrammes; `_get_or_create_subprogram` only fills blank texts, so live 2027 holds 23 unused programmes, 41 unused subprogrammes and subprogrammes with preprint rationale/goal texts — live 2027 subprogramme texts have been wrong since 30 Oct; (2) `approved_book_provenance()` recomputes `applied_hash` with today's serializer and can never verify a load by older code after later migrations; (3) 2026 catalogue ops, account administration and `pp_data_change period_id=1` were counted as 2027 writes.
- Decision: CIV-217 (lead-coded, PR #490). Content signatures reference programme/subprogramme by name; `catalogue_review` requires every ONAYLI identity, deletes only unreferenced non-ONAYLI rows (referenced → refusal), lists and replaces stale texts; provenance refusal dropped (marker + payload + per-directorate signatures remain); audit classification narrowed as above, `period_id=2`/ambiguous still refuse. Live-like local rehearsal: main reproduces every refusal class; CIV-217 plans 0 refusals and applies. 8 new tests; Codex (GPT-6.1 Sol, High) no major issues; CI green.
- Follow-up (not scheduled): the loader keeping existing subprogramme texts is a design gap for any future reload.
- Next: merge #490 (owner approval asked) → release phase5→main (owner merges, deploys, /health) → owner re-runs the read-only plan (expected 0 refusals; about 23 programmes + 41 subprogrammes as leftovers; stale texts listed) → fresh verified backup (<60 min) → apply with the phrase → lead checks the live result.
